<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that weak Ma–Trudinger–Wang curvature on a smooth, connected, compact Riemannian manifold of dimension at least two without boundary implies convexity of every tangent injectivity domain, resolving Villani's conjecture in this setting. Conjugate cut points are allowed. More generally, every ordinary subgradient of a squared-distance cost potential is a minimizing velocity with a global supporting mountain. No density hypothesis is used.

- **claim-main-convexity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): If $(M,g)$ satisfies <a href="#eq:weak-mtw" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weak-mtw">[eq:weak-mtw]</a>, then $I(x)$ is convex for every $x\in M$. More precisely, for every $v_0,v_1\in I(x)$ and $\theta\in[0,1]$, $$(1-\theta)v_0+\theta v_1\in I(x).$$
  - Explanation: Weak MTW curvature on a smooth connected compact boundaryless manifold of dimension at least two makes every open tangent injectivity domain linearly convex. Conjugate cut endpoints are allowed.
- **claim-global-foundation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-foundation)): Under <a href="#eq:weak-mtw" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weak-mtw">[eq:weak-mtw]</a>, every potential $u$, with its dual potential $v$, has the following properties.* 1. *Every $p\in\partial u(x)$ belongs to $G_x$ and satisfies $$u(x')\geq u(x)+c(x,\exp_xp)-c(x',\exp_xp) \qquad(x'\in M).$$ For every $0<t<1$, the map $F_t:\Gamma_u\to M$, $F_t(x,p)=\exp_x(tp)$, is a homeomorphism; $tp\in I(x)$ and $x$ is the unique global minimizing pole of $Q_tu$ at $F_t(x,p)$.* 2. *For every dual pair $(u,v)$, every $x$ and every $r$, the lifted section $$\mathcal S_x(r)=\left\{p\in G_x: u(x)+\frac{\|p\|^2}{2}+v(\exp_xp)\leq r\right\}$$ is compact and convex. If it is nonempty and $r\geq0$, then $$\operatorname{diam}\mathcal S_x(r)^2 \leq8\left(r+\mathop{\mathrm{osc}}_{p\in\mathcal S_x(r)}v(\exp_xp)\right).$$* 3. *For $0<t<1$, one has $Q_tu=-Q_{1-t}v\in C^{1,1}(M)$. Its $C^{1,1}$ seminorm and the Lipschitz constants of the pole and momentum maps $F_t^{-1}$ depend only on $M,g,t$. There are also $r_t,b_t>0$, depending only on $M,g,t$, such that, for $z=F_t(x,p)$, $$u(x')+\frac{c(x',z)}t \geq u(x)+\frac{c(x,z)}t+b_t d(x,x')^2 \quad\text{if }d(x,x')<r_t.$$ These constants can be chosen uniformly on compact subintervals of $(0,1)$.* *No density hypothesis is required.
  - Explanation: Under weak MTW every potential has global supports at its ordinary subgradients, homeomorphic intermediate projections with unique poles, compact convex lifted sections with a diameter bound, and uniform C^{1,1} intermediate regularity, with no density hypothesis.
- **claim-interior-characterization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-interior-characterization)): For every $x\in M$, $$I(x)\subset G_x,\qquad rG_x\subset I(x)\quad(0\leq r<1). \label{eq:radial-interior}$$ A velocity $w\in T_xM$ belongs to $I(x)$ if and only if it is the unique minimizing velocity from $x$ to $\exp_x w$ and is nonconjugate. The set $\mathcal I$ is open, and $c$ is smooth in a neighborhood of each pair $(x,\exp_x w)$ with $w\in I(x)$. There is a number $\rho>0$ such that $\|w\|_x<\rho$ implies $w\in I(x)$ for every $x$.
  - Explanation: The open injectivity domain sits inside the closed minimizing domain, contains its proper radial contractions, and consists exactly of the nonconjugate unique minimizing velocities. The total set is open and the cost is smooth there.
- **claim-hessian-index** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-hessian-index)): If $w\in G_x$ is nonconjugate, then, along $\gamma_{x,w}(\tau)=\exp_x(\tau w)$ on $[0,1]$, $$A_x(w)(\xi,\xi) =\min_{\substack{X(0)=\xi\\X(1)=0}} Q_{\gamma_{x,w}}(X,X). \label{eq:hessian-index-minimum}$$ The minimum is attained uniquely by the Jacobi field with these endpoints. In particular, $A_x(0)=g_x$.
  - Explanation: At a nonconjugate minimizing velocity the branch Hessian equals the minimum of the index form over fields with prescribed initial value and zero final value, attained by the Jacobi field.
- **claim-radial-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-radial-comparison)): For every $a\in G_x$ and $0<s<s'<1$, $$A_x(sa)>\frac{s}{s'}A_x(s'a) \label{eq:radial-comparison}$$ as quadratic forms; that is, the difference is positive on every nonzero vector.
  - Explanation: The divided branch Hessian decreases strictly along a radial ray as the duration grows, as quadratic forms.
- **claim-transverse-concavity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-transverse-concavity)): Let $b_t=b_0+t\eta$ be an affine velocity line in $T_xM$, and let $\xi\perp\eta$. On every interval on which $b_t\in I(x)$, $$\frac{d^2}{dt^2}A_x(b_t)(\xi,\xi) =-\frac23\mathfrak S_{(x,b_t)}(\xi,\eta)\leq0. \label{eq:transverse-concavity}$$ Consequently, if $b=\sum_{i=1}^m\theta_i b_i$, with $\theta_i\geq0$ and $\sum_i\theta_i=1$, the convex hull of the $b_i$ is contained in $I(x)$, and $\langle b_i,\xi\rangle_x$ is independent of $i$, then $$A_x(b)(\xi,\xi)\geq\sum_{i=1}^m\theta_iA_x(b_i)(\xi,\xi).$$
  - Explanation: Along an affine velocity line inside the injectivity domain the second derivative of the branch Hessian in a perpendicular test direction is a multiple of the MTW tensor and is nonpositive, which gives a Jensen inequality on convex hulls.
- **claim-segment-nonconjugacy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-segment-nonconjugacy)): Suppose that $[b_0,b_1]\subset G_x$ and that $b_0,b_1$ are nonconjugate. Then every velocity in $[b_0,b_1]$ is nonconjugate.
  - Explanation: A minimizing segment with nonconjugate endpoints contains only nonconjugate velocities. The proof combines transverse concavity, the radial Hessian identity and an index-form trial field.
- **claim-hull-nonconjugacy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-first-cut)): Let $a_1,\ldots,a_m\in T_xM$ be nonconjugate velocities, with $m\geq1$, and suppose $$K=\operatorname{co}\{a_1,\ldots,a_m\}\subset G_x.$$ Then every velocity in $K$ is nonconjugate.
  - Explanation: A finite convex hull of nonconjugate velocities that is minimizing consists entirely of nonconjugate velocities, by induction on the number of positive weights using the segment result.
- **claim-uniform-supports** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-prelim)): There is a geometric constant $C_g$ such that $c(\cdot,y)$ and $c(x,\cdot)$ are uniformly semiconcave, with constant $C_g$, including at cut points. Every potential is $D$-Lipschitz and uniformly semiconvex. If $$\mathcal V_u(x)=\{p\in G_x: u(x)+c(x,\exp_xp)+v(\exp_xp)=0\},$$ then $$\label{geo:active-hull} \partial u(x)=\operatorname{conv}\mathcal V_u(x).$$ The total active-log graph $\{(x,p):p\in\mathcal V_u(x)\}$ is compact. Every member of the convex hull in <a href="#geo:active-hull" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="geo:active-hull">[geo:active-hull]</a> has a representation with positive weights using at most $n+1$ affinely independent members of $\mathcal V_u(x)$.
  - Explanation: The squared-distance cost is uniformly semiconcave across cut points, potentials are Lipschitz and semiconvex, and every ordinary subgradient is the convex hull of active logs with a finite affinely independent representation.
- **claim-radial-divided-action** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-radial-index)): Let $p\in G_x$, and let $0<t<s<1$. Along $\gamma(r)=\exp_x(rp)$, let $S_p(r)$ be the Jacobi tensor with $S_p(0)=0$ and $D_rS_p(0)=\mathrm{Id}$, using parallel orthonormal frames. Then $$\label{geo:radial-derivative} \frac{d}{dr}H_r(p)=-S_p(r)^{-1}S_p(r)^{-T}, \qquad H_t(p)-H_s(p)\geq\kappa_g(s-t)\mathrm{Id}$$ for a constant $\kappa_g>0$ depending only on the manifold. Furthermore, $$\label{geo:radial-identity} A_x(w)w=w$$ on every smooth minimizing branch.
  - Explanation: The radial derivative of the divided source Hessian is given through the fundamental Jacobi tensor, giving a uniform positive gap between times, together with the radial identity on smooth branches.
- **claim-transverse-slack** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-slack)): Let $p_i\in G_x$, $m_i>0$, $\sum_i m_i=1$, and $$p=\sum_i m_ip_i,\qquad E=\mathop{\mathrm{span}}\{p_i-p\}.$$ Suppose $t\operatorname{conv}\{p_i\}\subset I(x)$ and $0<t<s<1$. Then $$\label{geo:transverse-slack} \left[\frac{A_x(tp)}t- \sum_i m_i\frac{A_x(sp_i)}s\right]\bigg\|_{E^\perp} \geq\kappa_g(s-t)\mathrm{Id}.$$ The same conclusion holds at a limiting time $t$ if the radially contracted hulls lie in $I(x)$ and the displayed Hessians have nonconjugate branch limits.
  - Explanation: Transverse Jensen concavity together with radial comparison gives a quantitative positive gap on the orthogonal complement of the span of the active velocity differences.
- **claim-short-time** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-short-time)): Let $f$ be Lipschitz and semiconvex. For sufficiently small $t>0$, depending only on its Lipschitz and semiconvexity bounds and on the manifold, the map $$F_t:\Gamma_f\longrightarrow M,\qquad \Gamma_f=\{(x,p):p\in\partial f(x)\},\qquad F_t(x,p)=\exp_x(tp),$$ is a homeomorphism. Its inverse is locally Lipschitz, as a map into the tangent bundle. The minimizing pole of $Q_tf$ is unique, $Q_tf\in C^{1,1}$, and $$\mathop{\mathrm{grad}}Q_tf(z)=-\frac1t\log_zx_t(z).$$ The compact graph $\Gamma_f$ is an $n$-dimensional Lipschitz manifold.
  - Explanation: For a Lipschitz semiconvex datum and small time, the subgradient graph projection is a homeomorphism with locally Lipschitz inverse, unique pole and C^{1,1} infimum convolution.
- **claim-minimizer-capture** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-minimizer-capture)): Let $u$ be a potential, let $t>0$, and let $z\in M$. If $x$ is a global minimizer of $u+c_t(\cdot,z)$, then $$\frac wt\in\partial u(x)\qquad\text{for every }w\in L_x(z).$$ Consequently, $F_t:\Gamma_u\to M$ is surjective for every $t>0$.
  - Explanation: Every minimizing velocity from a global minimizing pole, divided by the time, is an ordinary subgradient there, so the graph projection is surjective at every positive time.
- **claim-local-injectivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-local-injectivity)): Let $u$ be a potential and $0<t<1$. Suppose that $$rp\in I(x)\quad\text{for every }(x,p)\in\Gamma_u \text{ and }0<r<t,$$ and that every vector $tp$, $(x,p)\in\Gamma_u$, is nonconjugate. Then $F_t:\Gamma_u\to M$ is locally injective.
  - Explanation: If earlier scaled graph vectors lie in the injectivity domain and the vectors at the scale are nonconjugate, the projection is locally injective. Collisions are excluded by exact cancellation of linear terms before taking limits.
- **claim-covering-homeomorphism** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-covering-homeomorphism)): Let $X$ and $N$ be nonempty connected compact topological $n$-manifolds without boundary. A continuous locally injective map $f:X\to N$ that is homotopic to a homeomorphism $h:X\to N$ is itself a homeomorphism.
  - Explanation: A locally injective continuous self-map between compact connected manifolds without boundary that is homotopic to a homeomorphism is itself a homeomorphism, via invariance of domain and covering theory.
- **claim-global-support** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-support)): For every potential $u$, every $x\in M$, and every $p\in\partial u(x)$, one has $p\in G_x$ and $$\label{geo:global-support} u(x')\geq u(x)+c(x,\exp_xp)-c(x',\exp_xp) \qquad\text{for all }x'\in M.$$ For each $0<t<1$, the projection $F_t:\Gamma_u\to M$ is a homeomorphism; its vectors $tp$ lie in $I(x)$, and its poles are the unique global minimizers of $Q_tu$.
  - Explanation: Every ordinary subgradient is a minimizing velocity with a global supporting inequality, and for each time below one the projection is a homeomorphism whose poles are the unique global minimizers.
- **claim-convex-domains** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-convexity)): For every $x$, both $G_x$ and $I(x)$ are convex.
  - Explanation: Both the closed minimizing domain and the open injectivity domain are convex, obtained from the global support theorem applied to a maximum of two supports.
- **claim-convex-sections** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-sections)): Let $(u,v)$ be a dual pair and define $$\mathcal S_x(r)=\left\{p\in G_x: u(x)+\frac{\|p\|^2}{2}+v(\exp_xp)\leq r\right\}.$$ Every such set is compact and convex, including its ordinary and conjugate cut endpoints. If it is nonempty and $r\geq0$, then $$\label{geo:section-diameter} \operatorname{diam}\mathcal S_x(r)^2 \leq 8\left(r+ \mathop{\mathrm{osc}}_{p\in\mathcal S_x(r)}v(\exp_xp)\right).$$
  - Explanation: Lifted gap sections of a dual pair are compact and convex up to conjugate cut endpoints, and their squared diameter is bounded in terms of the level and the oscillation of the dual potential.
- **claim-intermediate-regularity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-intermediate)): Let $(u,v)$ be a dual pair and $0<t<1$. Then $$\label{geo:intermediate-duality} Q_tu=-Q_{1-t}v\in C^{1,1}(M).$$ The $C^{1,1}$ seminorm is bounded solely in terms of $M,g,t$. If $(x_t(z),p_t(z))=F_t^{-1}(z)$ and $\Phi_s$ denotes geodesic flow on $TM$, then $$\label{geo:pole-flow} (x_t(z),p_t(z)) =\Phi_{-t}(z,\mathop{\mathrm{grad}}Q_tu(z)), \qquad x_t(z)=\exp_z(-t\mathop{\mathrm{grad}}Q_tu(z)).$$ In particular the pole and momentum are Lipschitz, with constants depending only on $M,g,t$.* *There are also $r_t,b_t>0$, depending only on $M,g,t$, such that every projected pair $z=\exp_x(tp)$ satisfies $$\label{geo:uniform-growth} u(x')+c_t(x',z) \geq u(x)+c_t(x,z)+b_t d(x,x')^2 \qquad\text{if }d(x,x')<r_t.$$ These assertions use no density hypothesis. On every compact subinterval of $(0,1)$, their constants can be chosen uniformly.
  - Explanation: At intermediate times the infimum convolution of a potential equals minus that of the dual, is C^{1,1} with geometry-only bounds, has Lipschitz pole and momentum, and has uniform quadratic growth, without density hypotheses.
- **claim-local-growth** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-geo-local-growth)): Let $u$ be a potential and $0<t<1$. Suppose that, for every $(x,p)\in\Gamma_u$, one has $rp\in I(x)$ for $0<r<t$ and $tp$ is nonconjugate. Put $z=\exp_x(tp)$ and let $\hat c$ be the smooth action branch through $(x,tp)$. Then there are $b,\rho>0$ such that $$u(x')+\frac{\hat c(x',z)}t \geq u(x)+\frac{\hat c(x,z)}t+b\,d(x,x')^2 \qquad\text{if }d(x,x')<\rho.$$ In particular, $F_t:\Gamma_u\to M$ is open.
  - Explanation: Under nonconjugacy up to the scale and earlier scaled vectors in the injectivity domain, the action branch has local quadratic growth at the pole, so the graph projection is open.
- **claim-positive-degree** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-geo-degree)): Let $u$ be a potential and $0<t<1$. Assume that every $rp$, with $(x,p)\in\Gamma_u$ and $0<r\leq t$, is nonconjugate. At each $(x,p)$, put $z=\exp_x(tp)$ and let $\hat c$ be the smooth action branch determined by $tp$. Suppose that $x$ is a local minimum of $$x'\longmapsto u(x')+\frac{\hat c(x',z)}t,$$ and suppose that $F_t:\Gamma_u\to M$ is open. Then $F_t$ is a homeomorphism. Its pole at every endpoint is the unique global minimizing pole of $Q_tu$, and every $tp$ lies in $I(x)$.
  - Explanation: Nonconjugacy, local minimality of the action branch and openness give a nonnegative projection Jacobian and a degree argument showing the projection is a homeomorphism.

        "anchor": "stmt-thm-foundation",
        "exact_quote": "Under <a href=\"#eq:weak-mtw\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:weak-mtw\">[eq:weak-mtw]</a>, every potential $u$, with its dual potential $v$, has the following properties.*\n\n1.  *Every $p\\in\\partial u(x)$ belongs to $G_x$ and satisfies $$u(x')\\geq u(x)+c(x,\\exp_xp)-c(x',\\exp_xp)\n     \\qquad(x'\\in M).$$ For every $0<t<1$, the map $F_t:\\Gamma_u\\to M$, $F_t(x,p)=\\exp_x(tp)$, is a homeomorphism; $tp\\in I(x)$ and $x$ is the unique global minimizing pole of $Q_tu$ at $F_t(x,p)$.*\n\n2.  *For every dual pair $(u,v)$, every $x$ and every $r$, the lifted section $$\\mathcal S_x(r)=\\left\\{p\\in G_x:\n     u(x)+\\frac{|p|^2}{2}+v(\\exp_xp)\\leq r\\right\\}$$ is compact and convex. If $$\\mathcal V_u(x)=\\{p\\in G_x:\n                 u(x)+c(x,\\exp_xp)+v(\\exp_xp)=0\\},$$ then $$\\label{geo:active-hull}\n \\partial u(x)=\\operatorname{conv}\\mathcal V_u(x).$$ The total active-log graph $\\{(x,p):p\\in\\mathcal V_u(x)\\}$ is compact. Every member of the convex hull in <a href=\"#geo:active-hull\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"geo:active-hull\">[geo:active-hull]</a> has a representation with positive weights using at most $n+1$ affinely independent members of $\\mathcal V_u(x)$.",
      "explanation": "The squared-distance cost is uniformly semiconcave across cut points, potentials are Lipschitz and semiconvex, and every ordinary subgradient is the convex hull of active logs with a finite affinely independent representation.",

        "anchor": "stmt-geo-short-time",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f$ be Lipschitz and semiconvex. Its inverse is locally Lipschitz, as a map into the tangent bundle. The minimizing pole of $Q_tf$ is unique, $Q_tf\\in C^{1,1}$, and $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{grad}}Q_tf(z)=-\\frac1t\\log_zx_t(z).$$ The compact graph $\\Gamma_f$ is an $n$-dimensional Lipschitz manifold.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $(u,v)$ be a dual pair and $0<t<1$. Then $$\\label{geo:intermediate-duality}\n Q_tu=-Q_{1-t}v\\in C^{1,1}(M).$$ The $C^{1,1}$ seminorm is bounded solely in terms of $M,g,t$. If $(x_t(z),p_t(z))=F_t^{-1}(z)$ and $\\Phi_s$ denotes geodesic flow on $TM$, then $$\\label{geo:pole-flow}\n (x_t(z),p_t(z))\n   =\\Phi_{-t}(z,\\mathop{\\mathrm{grad}}Q_tu(z)),\n \\qquad\n x_t(z)=\\exp_z(-t\\mathop{\\mathrm{grad}}Q_tu(z)).$$ In particular the pole and momentum are Lipschitz, with constants depending only on $M,g,t$.*\n\n\n*There are also $r_t,b_t>0$, depending only on $M,g,t$, such that every projected pair $z=\\exp_x(tp)$ satisfies $$\\label{geo:uniform-growth}\n u(x')+c_t(x',z)\n \\geq u(x)+c_t(x,z)+b_t d(x,x')^2\n \\qquad\\text{if }d(x,x')<r_t.$$ These assertions use no density hypothesis. On every compact subinterval of $(0,1)$, their constants can be chosen uniformly.",

      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-geo-intermediate",

If $(x_t(z),p_t(z))=F_t^{-1}(z)$ and $\\Phi_s$ denotes geodesic flow on $TM$, then $$\\label{geo:pole-flow}\n (x_t(z),p_t(z))\n   =\\Phi_{-t}(z,\\mathop{\\mathrm{grad}}Q_tu(z)),\n \\qquad\n x_t(z)=\\exp_z(-t\\mathop{\\mathrm{grad}}Q_tu(z)).$$ In particular the pole and momentum are Lipschitz, with constants depending only on $M,g,t$.*\n\n\n*There are also $r_t,b_t>0$, depending only on $M,g,t$, such that every projected pair $z=\\exp_x(tp)$ satisfies $$\\label{geo:uniform-growth}\n u(x')+c_t(x',z)\n \\geq u(x)+c_t(x,z)+b_t d(x,x')^2\n \\qquad\\text{if }d(x,x')<r_t.$$ These assertions use no density hypothesis. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Open tangent injectivity domain I(x)** ([source](#pm-h-3)): The open tangent injectivity domain is $$I(x)=\left\{v\in T_xM:\ \text{there is }a>1\text{ such that } d(x,\exp_x(av))=a\|v\|_x\right\}. \label{eq:injectivity-domain}$$
- **MTW tensor** ([source](#eq:mtw-definition)): Our MTW convention is $$\mathfrak S_{(x,v)}(\xi,\eta) =-\frac32\left. \frac{\partial^4}{\partial s^2\partial t^2} c\bigl(\exp_x(t\xi),\exp_x(v+s\eta)\bigr) \right\|_{s=t=0},\qquad v\in I(x). \label{eq:mtw-definition}$$
- **Squared-distance cost** ([source](#eq:weak-mtw)): Put $c(x,y)=d(x,y)^2/2$.
- **Closed minimizing domain G_x** ([source](#eq:weak-mtw)): Write $G_x=\{p\in T_xM:d(x,\exp_xp)=\|p\|_x\}$ for the closed minimizing domain.
- **Nonconjugate velocity** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): A velocity is called *nonconjugate* if the vertical differential $d_w\exp_x:T_xM\to T_{\exp_x w}M$ is nonsingular.
- **Potential and dual pair** ([source](#pm-h-4)): A *potential* is a function $u(x)=\max_y\{-c(x,y)-v(y)\}$ for a continuous datum $v:M\to\mathbb R$; replacing $v$ by the dual transform $v(y)=\max_x\{-c(x,y)-u(x)\}$ gives a dual pair with nonnegative gap $u(x)+c(x,y)+v(y)$.
- **Active target and active log** ([source](#pm-h-6)): A target is active at $x$ if it attains the maximum defining $u(x)$; an active log is any minimizing velocity to that target.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-dimension-bound | manifold dimension n | ≥ | $2$ | dimension n |
| q-injectivity-extension | extension factor a in the definition of I(x) | > | $1$ | velocities v in I(x) |
| q-radial-contraction | radial contraction factor r | < | $1$ | 0 ≤ r < 1 |
| q-mtw-prefactor | normalizing prefactor of the MTW tensor | = | $-\frac32$ | fixed convention |
| q-transverse-prefactor | coefficient relating second derivative of branch Hessian to the MTW tensor | = | $-\frac23$ | test vectors perpendicular to the line direction |
| q-section-diameter-constant | constant in the lifted-section diameter bound | ≤ | $8$ | level r and oscillation of v |
| q-active-log-count | number of affinely independent active logs in a subgradient representation | ≤ | $n+1$ | dimension n |
| q-radial-gap | gap between divided source Hessians along a ray | ≥ | $\kappa_g(s-t)\mathrm{Id}$ | constant kappa_g depending only on the manifold |
| q-hessian-upper | upper Hessian bound of the intermediate potential | ≤ | $\frac{C_g}{t}g$ | time t in (0,1) |
| q-hessian-lower | lower Hessian bound of the intermediate potential | ≥ | $-\frac{C_g}{1-t}g$ | time t in (0,1) |
| q-uniform-growth | quadratic growth of the infimum-convolution objective near the pole | ≥ | $b_t d(x,x')^2$ | r_t, b_t depend only on M, g, t |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-interior** [claim](#stmt-lem-interior-characterization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-interior-characterization): Minimizing curves have no corners; kernel Jacobi fields extended by zero contradict uniqueness; inverse branch of the exponential pair map gives openness. -> I(x) equals nonconjugate unique minimizing velocities; I open
- **step-hessian-index** [claim](#stmt-lem-hessian-index) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hessian-index): Variational Jacobi field gives the Hessian as an index form; minimality of the geodesic gives the minimum property. -> branch Hessian as minimal index form
- **step-radial-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-radial-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-radial-comparison): Extend the minimizing field by zero to a longer interval; strict inequality because the extension is not Jacobi. -> strict radial decrease of divided Hessian
- **step-transverse-concavity** [claim](#stmt-lem-transverse-concavity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transverse-concavity): Differentiate the Hessian identity twice along the velocity line to obtain the MTW tensor; apply weak MTW; Jensen by induction. -> concavity of the branch Hessian in transverse directions
- **step-segment-nonconjugacy** [claim](#stmt-prop-segment-nonconjugacy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-segment-nonconjugacy): Assume a singular interior velocity; trial Jacobi field gives singular growth of the Hessian; transverse concavity confines the kernel; radial identity gives a contradictory lower bound. -> no conjugate velocity on the segment
- **step-hull-nonconjugacy** [claim](#stmt-geo-first-cut) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-first-cut): Induction on the number of positive weights using the segment result. -> nonconjugacy of the whole minimizing hull
- **step-uniform-supports** [claim](#stmt-geo-prelim) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-prelim): Divided triangle inequality gives smooth upper supports; directional derivative formula and Carathéodory give the active-log hull. -> semiconcavity, Lipschitz bound and subgradient equals hull of active logs
- **step-radial-divided-action** [claim](#stmt-geo-radial-index) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-radial-index): Express the Hessian through fundamental Jacobi tensors and differentiate using Wronskian identities. -> radial derivative formula and uniform gap
- **step-transverse-slack** [claim](#stmt-geo-slack) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-slack): Jensen inequality from weak MTW in directions orthogonal to the span, then subtract using the radial gap. -> positive Hessian gap on the orthogonal complement
- **step-short-time** [claim](#stmt-geo-short-time) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-short-time): Strong convexity of the objective on a coordinate ball gives unique pole; coordinate estimate gives Lipschitz inverse. -> short-time homeomorphism with C^{1,1} infimum convolution
- **step-minimizer-capture** [claim](#stmt-geo-minimizer-capture) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-minimizer-capture): Action splitting gives a smooth upper support; first-order condition at a local minimum. -> every minimizing log divided by t is a subgradient; surjectivity
- **step-local-injectivity** [claim](#stmt-geo-local-injectivity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-local-injectivity): Contradiction with a colliding sequence; Taylor expansion with active slots; cancel linear terms at the finite stage; transverse slack gives a positive limit. -> local injectivity of the graph projection
- **step-covering** [claim](#stmt-lem-covering-homeomorphism) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-covering-homeomorphism): Invariance of domain gives a covering; the homotopy gives surjectivity on fundamental groups; path lifting forces injectivity. -> locally injective map homotopic to a homeomorphism is a homeomorphism
- **step-global-support** [claim](#stmt-geo-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-support): Take the first scale where a scaled graph vector leaves the injectivity domain; nonconjugacy, local injectivity and covering exclude this; pass to time one by closure. -> global support at every subgradient and homeomorphic projections below time one
- **step-convex-domains** [claim](#stmt-geo-convexity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-convexity): Apply global support to a maximum of two supports; the interior of a convex set is convex. -> convexity of closed and open domains
- **step-main-convexity** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#stmt-geo-convexity): Main theorem is the open-domain part of the convexity corollary. -> convexity of I(x) under weak MTW
- **step-convex-sections** [claim](#stmt-geo-sections) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-sections): Support theorem on a maximum of two supports gives convexity of sublevels; midpoint identity gives the diameter bound. -> compact convex sections with diameter bound
- **step-intermediate-regularity** [claim](#stmt-geo-intermediate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-intermediate): Divided triangle inequality gives duality; two-sided supports give C^{1,1}; geodesic flow reversal gives Lipschitz poles; split support gives quadratic growth. -> intermediate regularity and pole control
- **step-global-foundation** [claim](#stmt-thm-foundation) / [proof](#stmt-thm-foundation): The three items are the support theorem, the section corollary and the intermediate proposition. -> all properties of the foundation theorem
- **step-local-growth** [claim](#stmt-geo-local-growth) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-local-growth): Active-ray expansion with the limiting slack inequality gives quadratic growth; minimizers on a small ball give openness. -> local quadratic growth and openness
- **step-positive-degree** [claim](#stmt-geo-degree) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-degree): Minty coordinates give a nonnegative Jacobian; degree identity and area formula exclude two overlapping open images. -> projection is a homeomorphism

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-convex-injectivity-domain**: On a compact boundaryless Riemannian manifold satisfying the weak transverse curvature condition, the open tangent injectivity domain at every base point is convex, even when conjugate cut endpoints occur.
- **inv-global-support-subgradient**: Every ordinary subgradient of a squared-distance cost potential is a minimizing velocity and supports the potential globally from below, with no density assumption.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-time-one-inverse-projection**: Inverting the projection from the subgradient graph, or using the smooth regularisation, at time one. -> At time one a potential can have many poles with the same endpoint, so the inverse projection is not defined.
- **anti-boundary-noncompact**: Applying the covering argument to manifolds with boundary or to noncompact spaces. -> The covering-to-homeomorphism step needs compact connected manifolds without boundary.
- **anti-weak-mtw-unchecked**: Concluding convexity of injectivity domains on a manifold where the transverse curvature condition is not verified. -> The convexity conclusion is derived from the transverse condition; without it the argument gives nothing.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The results are existence and topological statements about injectivity domains and subgradient graphs on a compact manifold (convexity, homeomorphism, global support); the paper gives no finite procedure with explicit steps that computes an output, so no runnable reference with an independent oracle exists.

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **lim-weak-mtw-hypothesis** (hypothesis): Every conclusion is derived from the weak transverse curvature condition imposed on all base points and all velocities of the injectivity domain. Nothing is claimed for metrics where it is not verified. Blocked use: ['Asserting convexity of injectivity domains on a manifold where the transverse curvature condition is unchecked or fails']
- **lim-compact-boundaryless** (hypothesis): The setting is a smooth, connected, compact Riemannian manifold without boundary of dimension at least two. The covering step is stated only for compact connected manifolds without boundary. Blocked use: ['Applying the covering argument to manifolds with boundary', 'Applying it to noncompact manifolds']
- **lim-time-one-poles** (edge_case): At time one an arbitrary potential can have many poles sharing one endpoint, so inverse projection and smooth regularization are only available for times strictly between zero and one. Only minimizing-vector and supporting inequalities pass to time one. Blocked use: ['Inverting the subgradient graph projection at time one', 'Using the C^{1,1} regularization at time one']
- **lim-nonconstructive-continuation** (nonconstructive): The continuation argument works by contradiction at a first failing scale found by compactness, together with invariance of domain and covering theory. It gives no procedure with stated cost. Blocked use: ['Quoting a running time or algorithm for computing injectivity domains from this paper']
- **lim-geometric-constants** (approximation): The regularity constants depend only on the manifold, its metric and the time, and are asserted to exist without explicit values. The gap constant in the radial comparison is likewise not computed. Blocked use: ['Using the constants as explicit numerical tolerances in a computation']
- **lim-degree-conditional** (formal_scope): The positive-degree route is conditional on nonconjugacy of all scaled vectors, local minimality of the action branch and openness of the projection. It is an optional alternative and is not used in the main proof. Blocked use: ['Treating the degree lemma as unconditional', 'Citing it as part of the main argument']
- **lim-no-strictness** (edge_case): Convexity is ordinary linear convexity only. No strict convexity or regularity of the boundary is asserted. Blocked use: ['Assuming a smooth or strictly convex boundary of the injectivity domain']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 360 (`lean/docs/360.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Global Support and Convex Injectivity Domains under Weak MTW](../../preprints/Global-Support-and-Convex-Injectivity-Domains-under-Weak-MTW-September-25-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Uniform Bi-Hölder Transport from Weak MTW](../../preprints/Uniform-Bi-Holder-Transport-from-Weak-MTW-September-25-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> On a compact connected smooth Riemannian manifold of dimension at least two, the formalized result proves that weak MTW curvature implies convexity of every open tangent injectivity domain. Prior convexity or nonfocality is not assumed. Additional formalized results give global support at ordinary subgradients, compact convex lifted gap sections with the stated diameter bound, and scale and local-injectivity estimates for finite target families. The latter finite-family result does not cover the paper's arbitrary-potential local-injectivity assertion.
>
> The formalized result gives uniform bi-Hölder optimal transport for squared-distance cost on each fixed compact connected smooth Riemannian manifold of dimension at least two satisfying weak MTW. For probability densities between fixed positive lower and finite upper bounds, one exponent and constant work for every pair of densities. The optimal map is a homeomorphism, is unique almost everywhere, and both it and its inverse satisfy the global Hölder bound. The constants are uniform over the density class on the fixed manifold, not over varying metrics.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace WeakMTWGlobalSupport

open scoped Manifold ContDiff Topology
open Set Filter Manifold Bundle

namespace WeakMTW

noncomputable section

abbrev Model (n : ℕ) := EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin n)
abbrev model (n : ℕ) := 𝓘(ℝ, Model n)

variable {n : ℕ} {M : Type*} [MetricSpace M] [ChartedSpace (Model n) M]
  [IsManifold (model n) ∞ M]
  [RiemannianBundle (fun x : M => TangentSpace (model n) x)]
  [IsContMDiffRiemannianBundle (model n) ∞ (Model n)
    (fun x : M => TangentSpace (model n) x)]
  [IsRiemannianManifold (model n) M]

def CompleteGeodesicWithInitialData (x : M) (v : TangentSpace (model n) x)
    (γ : ℝ → M) : Prop :=
  ContMDiff 𝓘(ℝ, ℝ) (model n) ∞ γ ∧
  ∃ h : γ 0 = x,
    (h ▸ mfderiv 𝓘(ℝ, ℝ) (model n) γ 0 (1 : ℝ)) = v ∧
    ∀ t : ℝ, ∃ ε : ℝ, 0 < ε ∧
      ∀ s ∈ Metric.ball t ε, ∀ r ∈ Metric.ball t ε,
        dist (γ s) (γ r) = ‖v‖ * |s - r|

def ExpEndpoint (x : M) (v : TangentSpace (model n) x) (y : M) : Prop :=
  ∃ γ : ℝ → M, CompleteGeodesicWithInitialData x v γ ∧ γ 1 = y

noncomputable def exp (x : M) (v : TangentSpace (model n) x) : M :=
  by
    classical
    exact if h : ∃ y, ExpEndpoint x v y then Classical.choose h else x

def injectivityDomain (x : M) : Set (TangentSpace (model n) x) :=
  {v | ∃ a : ℝ, 1 < a ∧ dist x (exp x (a • v)) = a * ‖v‖}

def cost (x y : M) : ℝ := dist x y ^ 2 / 2

noncomputable def mtw (x : M) (v ξ η : TangentSpace (model n) x) : ℝ :=
  -(3 / 2 : ℝ) * deriv (fun s : ℝ =>
    deriv (fun s' : ℝ => deriv (fun t : ℝ =>
      deriv (fun t' : ℝ => cost (exp x (t' • ξ)) (exp x (v + s' • η))) t) 0) s) 0

def HasWeakMTW : Prop :=
  ∀ (x : M) (v : TangentSpace (model n) x), v ∈ injectivityDomain x →
    ∀ ξ η : TangentSpace (model n) x, inner ℝ ξ η = 0 → 0 ≤ mtw x v ξ η

def MainConvexity : Prop :=
  ∀ x : M, Convex ℝ (injectivityDomain (n := n) x)

end

end WeakMTW

/-- Weak MTW implies linear convexity of every open tangent injectivity domain. -/
theorem current_main_convexity
    {n : ℕ} (hn : 2 ≤ n) {M : Type*}
    [MetricSpace M] [ChartedSpace (WeakMTW.Model n) M]
    [IsManifold (WeakMTW.model n) ∞ M]
    [CompactSpace M] [ConnectedSpace M]
    [RiemannianBundle (fun x : M => TangentSpace (WeakMTW.model n) x)]
    [IsContMDiffRiemannianBundle (WeakMTW.model n) ∞ (WeakMTW.Model n)
      (fun x : M => TangentSpace (WeakMTW.model n) x)]
    [IsRiemannianManifold (WeakMTW.model n) M]
    (hmtw : WeakMTW.HasWeakMTW (n := n) (M := M)) :
    WeakMTW.MainConvexity (n := n) (M := M) :=
  by
    sorry

end WeakMTWGlobalSupport

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that weak Ma–Trudinger–Wang curvature on a smooth, connected, compact Riemannian manifold of dimension at least two without boundary implies convexity of every tangent injectivity domain, resolving Villani's conjecture in this setting. Conjugate cut points are allowed. More generally, every ordinary subgradient of a squared-distance cost potential is a minimizing velocity with a global supporting mountain. No density hypothesis is used.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>

| [stmt-thm-foundation](#stmt-thm-foundation) | theorem | `thm:foundation` | 2 | Under [eq:weak-mtw], every potential $u$, with its dual potential $v$, has the following properties.* |
| [stmt-lem-interior-characterization](#stmt-lem-interior-characterization) | lemma | `lem:interior-characterization` | 3 | For every $x\in M$, $$I(x)\subset G_x,\qquad rG_x\subset I(x)\quad(0\leq r<1). |
| [stmt-lem-hessian-index](#stmt-lem-hessian-index) | lemma | `lem:hessian-index` | 4 | If $w\in G_x$ is nonconjugate, then, along $\gamma_{x,w}(\tau)=\exp_x(\tau w)$ on $[0,1]$, |
| [stmt-lem-radial-comparison](#stmt-lem-radial-comparison) | lemma | `lem:radial-comparison` | 5 | For every $a\in G_x$ and $0<s<s'<1$, |

| [stmt-prop-segment-nonconjugacy](#stmt-prop-segment-nonconjugacy) | proposition | `prop:segment-nonconjugacy` | 7 | Suppose that $[b_0,b_1]\subset G_x$ and that $b_0,b_1$ are nonconjugate. |

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

The squared geodesic distance is smooth before the cut locus, but optimal transport and its supporting functions naturally reach that boundary. The weak Ma–Trudinger–Wang condition controls the cost Hessian only in directions perpendicular to a velocity segment. We prove that this transverse condition already forces global convexity and supporting geometry at arbitrary minimizing endpoints, including conjugate cut points.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Weak MTW and convexity

<a id="eq:mtw-definition"></a>
<a id="eq:weak-mtw"></a>
Let $(M,g)$ be a smooth, connected, compact Riemannian manifold without boundary, of dimension $n\geq2$. Write $d$ for its geodesic distance, $\exp_x:T_xM\to M$ for its exponential map, and $|\cdot|_x$ and $\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_x$ for the norm and inner product of $g_x$. The open tangent injectivity domain is <a id="eq:injectivity-domain"></a>$$I(x)=\left\{v\in T_xM:\ \text{there is }a>1\text{ such that }
 d(x,\exp_x(av))=a|v|_x\right\}.
 \label{eq:injectivity-domain}$$ Its geodesics remain minimizing beyond time one. Put $c(x,y)=d(x,y)^2/2$. Our MTW convention is $$\mathfrak S_{(x,v)}(\xi,\eta)
 =-\frac32\left.
 \frac{\partial^4}{\partial s^2\partial t^2}
 c\bigl(\exp_x(t\xi),\exp_x(v+s\eta)\bigr)
 \right|_{s=t=0},\qquad v\in I(x).
 \label{eq:mtw-definition}$$ The cost is smooth near the pairs used here, as recalled in <a href="#sec:preliminaries" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:preliminaries">2</a>. Throughout the paper we assume precisely $$\langle\xi,\eta\rangle_x=0
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 \mathfrak S_{(x,v)}(\xi,\eta)\geq0
 \qquad(x\in M,\ v\in I(x),\ \xi,\eta\in T_xM).
 \label{eq:weak-mtw}$$ No condition on nonorthogonal pairs, strict lower bound, nonfocality, or prior convexity of $I(x)$ is assumed. Write $G_x=\{p\in T_xM:d(x,\exp_xp)=|p|_x\}$ for the closed minimizing domain.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Convexity of injectivity domains). *If $(M,g)$ satisfies <a href="#eq:weak-mtw" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weak-mtw">[eq:weak-mtw]</a>, then $I(x)$ is convex for every $x\in M$. More precisely, for every $v_0,v_1\in I(x)$ and $\theta\in[0,1]$, $$(1-\theta)v_0+\theta v_1\in I(x).$$*

</div>

In fact the closed minimizing domain $G_x$ is convex as well (<a href="#geo:convexity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geo:convexity">17</a>). The conclusion is ordinary linear convexity; no strict convexity or regularity of the boundary is asserted.

Villani proposed that weak MTW should force convexity of every tangent injectivity domain (Villani 2011, sec. 4.2) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Villani2011](#ref-Villani2011)]. In the compact setting of <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, the conjecture imposes neither nonfocality nor prior convexity. Figalli, Gallouët and Rifford proved the implication for nonfocal manifolds (Figalli, Gallouët, and Rifford 2015, Theorem 1.7) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[FGR2014](#ref-FGR2014)], where minimizing geodesics reach cut strictly before their first conjugate point. Here and below the numbering in that citation refers to the published article. [thm:main](#thm:main) removes this restriction: conjugate cut endpoints are allowed in every dimension $n\geq2$.

Loeper and Villani connected MTW to cut geometry, obtaining convexity under strong MTW and nonfocality and treating some focal configurations under stronger geometric conditions (Loeper and Villani 2010) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[LoeperVillani2010](#ref-LoeperVillani2010)]; see also (Figalli 2010, sec. 3.5.2) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Figalli2010](#ref-Figalli2010)]. Their work and the nonfocal theorem of (Figalli, Gallouët, and Rifford 2015) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[FGR2014](#ref-FGR2014)] distinguish the two obstructions in the present problem. The cost may cease to be smooth along a proposed velocity segment, and weak MTW controls only directions perpendicular to that segment. Neither difficulty can be removed by applying smooth-cost concavity beyond its domain of definition.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Global support and intermediate geometry

A *potential* is a function $u(x)=\max_y\{-c(x,y)-v(y)\}$ for a continuous datum $v:M\to\mathbb R$; replacing $v$ by the dual transform $v(y)=\max_x\{-c(x,y)-u(x)\}$ gives a dual pair with nonnegative gap $u(x)+c(x,y)+v(y)$. Such functions are Lipschitz and semiconvex. We use their ordinary semiconvex subdifferentials $\partial u(x)$, identifying vectors and covectors by the metric. Define $$\Gamma_u=\{(x,p):p\in\partial u(x)\},\qquad
 Q_tf(z)=\min_x\left\{f(x)+\frac{c(x,z)}t\right\}.$$

<a id="stmt-thm-foundation"></a>
<div id="thm:foundation" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2** (Global support and intermediate geometry). *Under <a href="#eq:weak-mtw" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weak-mtw">[eq:weak-mtw]</a>, every potential $u$, with its dual potential $v$, has the following properties.*

1.  *Every $p\in\partial u(x)$ belongs to $G_x$ and satisfies $$u(x')\geq u(x)+c(x,\exp_xp)-c(x',\exp_xp)
     \qquad(x'\in M).$$ For every $0<t<1$, the map $F_t:\Gamma_u\to M$, $F_t(x,p)=\exp_x(tp)$, is a homeomorphism; $tp\in I(x)$ and $x$ is the unique global minimizing pole of $Q_tu$ at $F_t(x,p)$.*

2.  *For every dual pair $(u,v)$, every $x$ and every $r$, the lifted section $$\mathcal S_x(r)=\left\{p\in G_x:
     u(x)+\frac{|p|^2}{2}+v(\exp_xp)\leq r\right\}$$ is compact and convex. If it is nonempty and $r\geq0$, then $$\operatorname{diam}\mathcal S_x(r)^2
     \leq8\left(r+\mathop{\mathrm{osc}}_{p\in\mathcal S_x(r)}v(\exp_xp)\right).$$*

3.  *For $0<t<1$, one has $Q_tu=-Q_{1-t}v\in C^{1,1}(M)$. Its $C^{1,1}$ seminorm and the Lipschitz constants of the pole and momentum maps $F_t^{-1}$ depend only on $M,g,t$. There are also $r_t,b_t>0$, depending only on $M,g,t$, such that, for $z=F_t(x,p)$, $$u(x')+\frac{c(x',z)}t
     \geq u(x)+\frac{c(x,z)}t+b_t d(x,x')^2
     \quad\text{if }d(x,x')<r_t.$$ These constants can be chosen uniformly on compact subintervals of $(0,1)$.*

*No density hypothesis is required.*

</div>

The detailed statements and proofs are [geo:support,geo:sections,geo:intermediate] [geo:support](#geo:support), [geo:sections](#geo:sections), [geo:intermediate](#geo:intermediate). The distinction between intermediate times and time one is essential: only the support and minimizing-velocity assertions pass to time one. For instance, $u(x)=-c(x,y_0)$ has many poles with the same final endpoint.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Supporting geometry and earlier methods

Ma, Trudinger and Wang introduced a strictly positive curvature condition in optimal-transport regularity theory (Ma, Trudinger, and Wang 2005) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[MTW2005](#ref-MTW2005)]; Trudinger and Wang treated its degenerate, nonnegative form, usually denoted A3w (Trudinger and Wang 2009, sec. 1) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[TW2009](#ref-TW2009)]. Loeper established necessity for continuity in the smooth-cost setting and developed the supporting-mountain principle (Loeper 2009) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Loeper2009](#ref-Loeper2009)]. Kim and McCann gave its geometric formulation and a double-mountain principle under smooth-cost and illuminated-set hypotheses (Kim and McCann 2010, Theorem 4.10) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[KM2010](#ref-KM2010)]. For squared distance on a compact manifold, Figalli, Rifford and Villani obtained the global supporting principle, including endpoint closure, from weak MTW together with convex injectivity domains (Figalli, Rifford, and Villani 2011, Lemma 3.1) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[FRV2011](#ref-FRV2011)]. The issue here is to derive that convexity rather than assume it.

Several ingredients precede the MTW theory. The shortened-distance support inequality is (Cordero-Erausquin, McCann, and Schmuckenschläger 2001, Claim 2.4) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[CMS2001](#ref-CMS2001)]. The endpoint-moving Jacobi trial used at a possible conjugate cut is related to the second-variation argument in (Cordero-Erausquin, McCann, and Schmuckenschläger 2001, Proposition 2.5) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[CMS2001](#ref-CMS2001)]; the transverse MTW reduction and the joint radial–tangential growth contradiction are proved here. Intermediate injectivity at differentiability points and inverse estimates on compact subsets of a positive-Hessian set appear in (Cordero-Erausquin, McCann, and Schmuckenschläger 2001, Lemma 5.3 and Claim 5.6) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[CMS2001](#ref-CMS2001)]. Our conclusion concerns the entire ordinary subgradient graph and gives bounds depending only on the geometry and the intermediate time.

The graph coordinates in the optional degree argument and the infimum regularization also have classical antecedents. Moreau developed quadratic inf-convolution and proximal decomposition (Moreau 1965, secs. 3–5, 7 and 12) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Moreau1965](#ref-Moreau1965)]; Minty’s monotone-graph coordinates are presented in (Alberti and Ambrosio 1999, Proposition 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[AA1999](#ref-AA1999)] and adapted to transport-plan rectifiability in (McCann, Pass, and Warren 2012, sec. 2) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[MPW2012](#ref-MPW2012)]. We prove the local semiconvex graph representation needed here. These constructions do not by themselves supply continuation through cut or the global support theorem. The resulting density-free geometry is distinct from the additional measure estimates needed for regularity of transport maps.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### The continuation argument

develops the minimizing-branch Hessian and index form without nonfocality. Proper radial shortening supplies smooth distance supports, and the divided Hessian decreases strictly with duration. These estimates also control the uniform constants in the intermediate conclusions.

The proof works on the entire ordinary subgradient graph of an arbitrary potential. A target is active at $x$ if it attains the maximum defining $u(x)$; an active log is any minimizing velocity to that target. Every ordinary subgradient is a convex combination of finitely many active logs. All minimizing logarithms of each active target are retained. These are finite local representations; the potential itself need not have finitely many targets.

At a first failing scale, limits from earlier scales show that the relevant finite convex hulls are still minimizing. The argument of <a href="#sec:conjugacy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:conjugacy">3</a> excludes conjugacy there. Transverse concavity confines a hypothetical conjugacy kernel to the segment direction; the radial Hessian identity then contradicts the growth forced by a Jacobi trial field.

To exclude local collisions of the graph projection, the finite active representations are compared before taking limits. The first-order inequalities force the positive-weight limiting velocities into an affine hyperplane perpendicular to the collision direction. The linear terms are then canceled exactly at the finite stage, before division by the squared collision distance and passage to the limit. Weak MTW and strict radial comparison give the contradiction. Invariance of domain now makes the projection a local homeomorphism. Compactness and a homotopy to the short-time graph homeomorphism make this covering one-sheeted. Capturing every minimizing logarithm excludes ordinary cuts and continues the construction to every time strictly below one. Closure yields global support at time one; convex lifted sections and two-sided intermediate supports give the remaining conclusions of <a href="#thm:foundation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:foundation">2</a>.

An optional alternative in <a href="#sec:degree-alternative" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:degree-alternative">5</a> replaces local injectivity by local quadratic growth and openness. A Jacobian-sign calculation, degree one, and the area formula then give the same conditional globalization. It reuses the common setup and is not needed for the main proof.

<a id="sec:preliminaries"></a>
### Minimizing velocities and the branch Hessian

Throughout the remaining sections we assume the weak MTW condition <a href="#eq:weak-mtw" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weak-mtw">[eq:weak-mtw]</a>. A velocity is called *nonconjugate* if the vertical differential $d_w\exp_x:T_xM\to T_{\exp_x w}M$ is nonsingular. We write $$\begin{aligned}
 L_x(q)&=\{w\in T_xM:\exp_x w=q,\ |w|_x=d(x,q)\},\\
 G_x&=\{w\in T_xM:w\in L_x(\exp_x w)\},\\
 \mathcal C&=\{(x,w)\in TM:w\in G_x\},
 \qquad
 \mathcal I=\{(x,w)\in TM:w\in I(x)\},
\end{aligned}$$ and set $D=\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}(M)$. Existence of minimizing geodesics shows that $L_x(q)$ is nonempty. Every velocity in $\mathcal C$ has norm at most $D$. Continuity of the distance and the exponential map shows that $\mathcal C$ is closed; as the base manifold is compact, $\mathcal C$ is therefore compact.

<a id="eq:index-form"></a>
We first record the index-form facts used below. For geometric background, see (Calegari 2015, secs. 5.7–5.8) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Calegari2013](#ref-Calegari2013)]; for the unrestricted energy second-variation formula, see (Gorodski 2016, Proposition 5.3.8) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Gorodski2016](#ref-Gorodski2016)]. The latter is the revised course chapter, whose formula allows tangential as well as normal variation fields. Along a geodesic $\gamma:[0,T]\to M$, let $$Q_\gamma(X,Y)=\int_0^T
 \bigl(\langle D_\tau X,D_\tau Y\rangle
       -\langle R(X,\dot\gamma)\dot\gamma,Y\rangle\bigr)\,d\tau .
 \label{eq:index-form}$$ Our curvature convention gives the Jacobi equation $D_\tau^2X+R(X,\dot\gamma)\dot\gamma=0$. Fields in <a href="#eq:index-form" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:index-form">[eq:index-form]</a> may be continuous and piecewise smooth. A constant-speed minimizing geodesic on $[0,T]$, with $T>0$, also minimizes the fixed-endpoint energy $E(\alpha)=\tfrac12\int_0^T|\dot\alpha|^2\,d\tau$. Indeed, Cauchy–Schwarz gives $$E(\alpha)\geq\frac{\operatorname{length}(\alpha)^2}{2T}
 \geq\frac{d(\gamma(0),\gamma(T))^2}{2T}=E(\gamma).$$ Energy second variation therefore gives $Q_\gamma(Z,Z)\geq0$ for every field with $Z(0)=Z(T)=0$, without a normality restriction. Approximation gives the same assertion for piecewise smooth fields. Moreover, equality forces $Z$ to be a Jacobi field. Indeed, nonnegativity applied to $Z+tW$ implies $Q_\gamma(Z,W)=0$ for every endpoint-zero test field $W$. Integration by parts first gives the Jacobi equation on each smooth piece and then continuity of $D_\tau Z$ at the break points. Thus $Z$ is a Jacobi field on the entire interval.

<a id="stmt-lem-interior-characterization"></a>
<div id="lem:interior-characterization" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:radial-interior"></a>
**Lemma 3** (Interior characterization). *For every $x\in M$, $$I(x)\subset G_x,\qquad rG_x\subset I(x)\quad(0\leq r<1).
 \label{eq:radial-interior}$$ A velocity $w\in T_xM$ belongs to $I(x)$ if and only if it is the unique minimizing velocity from $x$ to $\exp_x w$ and is nonconjugate. The set $\mathcal I$ is open, and $c$ is smooth in a neighborhood of each pair $(x,\exp_x w)$ with $w\in I(x)$. There is a number $\rho>0$ such that $|w|_x<\rho$ implies $w\in I(x)$ for every $x$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-interior-characterization"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-interior-characterization</summary>

*Proof.* The first inclusion in <a href="#eq:radial-interior" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:radial-interior">[eq:radial-interior]</a> follows from the definition. For $0<r<1$, a proper initial portion of the minimizing geodesic with velocity $w\in G_x$ remains minimizing up to time $1/r>1$ when parametrized with initial velocity $rw$. Hence $rw\in I(x)$. The case $r=0$ follows from the constant geodesic.

Suppose $w\in I(x)$, and put $\gamma(\tau)=\exp_x(\tau w)$. There is a $T>1$ for which $\gamma|_{[0,T]}$ is minimizing. Any other minimizing geodesic from $x$ to $\gamma(1)$, followed by $\gamma|_{[1,T]}$, would be a minimizing curve of the same length from $x$ to $\gamma(T)$. A minimizing curve has no corner, so its velocities agree at the joining point. Uniqueness for the geodesic equation then identifies the two initial geodesics. If $d_w\exp_x$ were singular, a nonzero kernel vector would give a nonzero Jacobi field on $[0,1]$ vanishing at both endpoints. Extend this field by zero to $[0,T]$. Its index form is zero, so the preceding observation makes the extension a Jacobi field on $[0,T]$. Its vanishing on $(1,T)$ contradicts uniqueness for the Jacobi equation. Thus $w$ is nonconjugate.

<a id="eq:exponential-pair-map"></a>
Conversely, suppose that $w$ is a unique minimizing velocity and is nonconjugate. The map $$\Psi:TM\longrightarrow M\times M,
 \qquad \Psi(z,u)=(z,\exp_z u)
 \label{eq:exponential-pair-map}$$ has an invertible differential at $(x,w)$. Choose neighborhoods on which it is a diffeomorphism. After shrinking its image neighborhood, every minimizing velocity for every pair in that neighborhood belongs to this inverse branch. To see this, a sequence of contrary minimizing velocities would have, by compactness of $\mathcal C$, a subsequential limit minimizing from $x$ to $\exp_x w$. Uniqueness makes this limit $w$, contradicting its exclusion from the open branch neighborhood. Every nearby pair has a minimizer, so its inverse-branch velocity is minimizing and is the unique minimizer. Thus an open neighborhood of $(x,w)$ in $TM$ consists of minimizing velocities. A small forward radial change of $w$ within this neighborhood proves $w\in I(x)$. For $w=0$, the same conclusion follows directly from the definition; also $L_x(x)=\{0\}$ and $d_0\exp_x=\mathrm{Id}$.

The inverse neighborhoods just constructed show that $\mathcal I$ is open. On their image neighborhoods the true cost is half the squared norm of the smooth inverse-branch velocity, so it is smooth. Finally, $\mathcal I$ contains the zero section. Its openness and compactness of the zero section give a uniform $\rho>0$ as asserted. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Smooth geodesic branches

<a id="eq:branch-hessian"></a>
At any nonconjugate $(x,w)$, the local inverse of <a href="#eq:exponential-pair-map" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:exponential-pair-map">[eq:exponential-pair-map]</a> defines a smooth branch cost $\hat c(z,q)$: it is half the squared norm of the inverse-branch velocity. Define the symmetric bilinear form $$A_x(w)=\mathop{\mathrm{Hess}}_{xx}\hat c(x,\exp_x w),
 \label{eq:branch-hessian}$$ where the second endpoint is held fixed in taking the covariant Hessian. Local inverse branches agree near a prescribed velocity, so $A$ depends smoothly on $(x,w)$ throughout the nonconjugate set. When $w\in I(x)$, the branch cost agrees locally with $c$ by <a href="#lem:interior-characterization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:interior-characterization">3</a>. At a nonconjugate minimizing cut velocity its central value is still $c(x,\exp_x w)$, and <a href="#eq:branch-hessian" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:branch-hessian">[eq:branch-hessian]</a> defines its smooth geodesic-branch Hessian. In particular, as velocities in $\mathcal I$ approach a nonconjugate minimizing velocity, their true-cost Hessians converge to this branch Hessian. This assertion also holds with the base point varying, using local tangent-bundle coordinates.

<a id="eq:radial-hessian"></a>
First variation gives $\nabla_x\hat c(x,\exp_x w)=-w$. For small $\tau$, with $\exp_x w$ fixed, the branch velocity at $\gamma_{x,w}(\tau)=\exp_x(\tau w)$ is $(1-\tau)\dot\gamma_{x,w}(\tau)$. Its negative has covariant derivative $w$ at $\tau=0$. Consequently, $$A_x(w)(w,\cdot)=\langle w,\cdot\rangle_x.
 \label{eq:radial-hessian}$$ This identity is valid at every nonconjugate velocity.

<a id="stmt-lem-hessian-index"></a>
<div id="lem:hessian-index" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:hessian-index-minimum"></a>
**Lemma 4** (Index-form representation). *If $w\in G_x$ is nonconjugate, then, along $\gamma_{x,w}(\tau)=\exp_x(\tau w)$ on $[0,1]$, $$A_x(w)(\xi,\xi)
 =\min_{\substack{X(0)=\xi\\X(1)=0}} Q_{\gamma_{x,w}}(X,X).
 \label{eq:hessian-index-minimum}$$ The minimum is attained uniquely by the Jacobi field with these endpoints. In particular, $A_x(0)=g_x$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-hessian-index"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-hessian-index</summary>

*Proof.* Move $x$ along a geodesic with initial velocity $\xi$ and follow the inverse branch to the fixed endpoint $\exp_x w$. The variational field $Y$ is Jacobi and satisfies $Y(0)=\xi$, $Y(1)=0$. Differentiating the first-variation formula gives $$A_x(w)(\xi,\xi)=-\langle\xi,D_\tau Y(0)\rangle_x
                 =Q_{\gamma_{x,w}}(Y,Y),$$ where the final equality is integration by parts. There is no initial acceleration term because the moving initial point follows a geodesic. For any admissible $X$, put $Z=X-Y$. The endpoint conditions and the Jacobi equation give $Q(Y,Z)=0$, while minimality of the original geodesic gives $Q(Z,Z)\geq0$. Hence $Q(X,X)\geq Q(Y,Y)$. Equality makes $Z$ a Jacobi field vanishing at both endpoints; nonconjugacy forces $Z=0$. At $w=0$, the minimizing field is $Y(\tau)=(1-\tau)\xi$ along the constant geodesic, which gives $A_x(0)(\xi,\xi)=|\xi|_x^2$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-lem-radial-comparison"></a>
<div id="lem:radial-comparison" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5** (Strict radial comparison). *For every $a\in G_x$ and $0<s<s'<1$, <a id="eq:radial-comparison"></a>$$A_x(sa)>\frac{s}{s'}A_x(s'a)
 \label{eq:radial-comparison}$$ as quadratic forms; that is, the difference is positive on every nonzero vector.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-radial-comparison"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-radial-comparison</summary>

*Proof.* Both $sa$ and $s'a$ belong to $I(x)$ by <a href="#lem:interior-characterization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:interior-characterization">3</a>. Time rescaling in <a href="#eq:hessian-index-minimum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:hessian-index-minimum">[eq:hessian-index-minimum]</a> gives, along $\gamma_{x,a}$, $$\frac1s A_x(sa)(\xi,\xi)
 =\min_{\substack{X(0)=\xi\\X(s)=0}}Q_{\gamma_{x,a}|_{[0,s]}}(X,X).$$ Extend the unique minimizing field by zero to $[0,s']$. It is an admissible field for the corresponding minimum on $[0,s']$ and has the same index-form value. If $\xi\ne0$, it cannot be that minimum: otherwise <a href="#lem:hessian-index" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:hessian-index">4</a>, with time rescaled, would make the extension Jacobi, although it vanishes on $(s,s')$ and is nonzero at $0$. Thus $A_x(sa)(\xi,\xi)/s>A_x(s'a)(\xi,\xi)/s'$, proving the claim. For $a=0$ the same argument applies, and the difference is explicitly $(1-s/s')g_x$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-lem-transverse-concavity"></a>
<div id="lem:transverse-concavity" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:transverse-concavity"></a>
**Lemma 6** (Transverse concavity). *Let $b_t=b_0+t\eta$ be an affine velocity line in $T_xM$, and let $\xi\perp\eta$. On every interval on which $b_t\in I(x)$, $$\frac{d^2}{dt^2}A_x(b_t)(\xi,\xi)
 =-\frac23\mathfrak S_{(x,b_t)}(\xi,\eta)\leq0.
 \label{eq:transverse-concavity}$$ Consequently, if $b=\sum_{i=1}^m\theta_i b_i$, with $\theta_i\geq0$ and $\sum_i\theta_i=1$, the convex hull of the $b_i$ is contained in $I(x)$, and $\langle b_i,\xi\rangle_x$ is independent of $i$, then $$A_x(b)(\xi,\xi)\geq\sum_{i=1}^m\theta_iA_x(b_i)(\xi,\xi).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-transverse-concavity"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-transverse-concavity</summary>

*Proof.* For fixed $v\in I(x)$, the second derivative at $u=0$ of $c(\exp_x(u\xi),\exp_x v)$ is $A_x(v)(\xi,\xi)$, because $u\mapsto\exp_x(u\xi)$ is a geodesic. Differentiating this identity twice in the affine velocity direction $\eta$ and using <a href="#eq:mtw-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:mtw-definition">[eq:mtw-definition]</a> gives the equality in <a href="#eq:transverse-concavity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transverse-concavity">[eq:transverse-concavity]</a>. The inequality is exactly <a href="#eq:weak-mtw" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weak-mtw">[eq:weak-mtw]</a>. Each segment in the stated convex hull has direction perpendicular to $\xi$, so the resulting linewise concavity gives the finite Jensen inequality by induction. Vanishing test vectors or line directions cause no exception. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:conjugacy"></a>
### Excluding conjugacy in minimizing convex hulls

The continuation argument will encounter finite convex hulls whose every velocity is known to be minimizing. Their generators will be nonconjugate, but their other points may lie at cut. We show here that none of those points can be conjugate. This removes the focal obstruction before the next section addresses uniqueness of minimizing geodesics.

The essential step concerns a segment with nonconjugate endpoints. Transverse concavity first confines a hypothetical conjugacy kernel to the segment direction. The radial Hessian identity then controls that remaining direction and contradicts the singular growth forced by an index-form trial field. All uses of weak MTW occur at proper radial contractions, where the true cost is smooth.

<a id="stmt-prop-segment-nonconjugacy"></a>
<div id="prop:segment-nonconjugacy" class="proposition">

**Proposition 7**. *Suppose that $[b_0,b_1]\subset G_x$ and that $b_0,b_1$ are nonconjugate. Then every velocity in $[b_0,b_1]$ is nonconjugate.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-segment-nonconjugacy"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-segment-nonconjugacy</summary>

*Proof.* The assertion is immediate if $b_0=b_1$. Otherwise, suppose there is a singular interior velocity $z$, and write the segment as $$z+te,\qquad -l_0\leq t\leq l_1,
 \qquad |e|_x=1,\quad l_0,l_1>0.$$ Here $b_0=z-l_0e$ and $b_1=z+l_1e$. The velocity $z$ is nonzero, since $d_0\exp_x=\mathrm{Id}$. For every $0<r<1$, the full contracted segment $\{r(z+te):-l_0\leq t\leq l_1\}$ is contained in $I(x)$ by <a href="#eq:radial-interior" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:radial-interior">[eq:radial-interior]</a>.

Choose $0\ne k\in\ker d_z\exp_x$. Variation of the initial velocity in the direction $k$ gives a Jacobi field $J_z$ along $\gamma_{x,z}$ on $[0,1]$ with $$J_z(0)=J_z(1)=0,\qquad D_\tau J_z(0)=k.$$ The function $\langle J_z,\dot\gamma_{x,z}\rangle$ is affine in $\tau$ and vanishes at both endpoints. Its initial derivative is $\langle k,z\rangle_x$, so $k\perp z$.

*A trial-field estimate.* The Jacobi-field trial below is related to the conjugate-cut argument of Cordero-Erausquin, McCann and Schmuckenschläger (Cordero-Erausquin, McCann, and Schmuckenschläger 2001, Proposition 2.5) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[CMS2001](#ref-CMS2001)]. We derive the needed Hessian estimate explicitly and combine it with transverse MTW concavity and the radial identity.

Fix a vector $\xi\in T_xM$ such that $\langle\xi,k\rangle_x\ne0$. For a velocity $a$ near $z$, let $T_a(\tau)$ denote parallel transport from $x$ along $\gamma_{x,a}$ to time $\tau$, and define $$j(\tau)=T_z(\tau)^{-1}J_z(\tau),\qquad
 J_a(\tau)=T_a(\tau)j(\tau),\qquad
 E_a(\tau)=(1-\tau)T_a(\tau)\xi.$$ The geodesic and parallel-transport equations give smooth dependence on $(a,\tau)$. Thus these are smooth families of fields along the parametrized geodesics, with $J_a(0)=J_a(1)=0$ and $E_a(0)=\xi$, $E_a(1)=0$. Every $J_a$ is nonzero, since its components $j(\tau)$ are fixed and not identically zero. The construction uses no inverse of the exponential map at $z$.

<a id="eq:trial-hessian"></a>
Write $Q_a=Q_{\gamma_{x,a}}$ on $[0,1]$ and set $P(a)=Q_a(J_a,J_a)$. This is a smooth function on a full neighborhood of $z$, and $P(z)=0$ by the Jacobi equation. It is nonnegative when $a\in G_x$. When $a\in I(x)$, it is strictly positive: equality would make the nonzero field $J_a$ a Jacobi field with zero endpoints, contrary to nonconjugacy. At $a=z$, integration by parts gives $$Q_z(E_z,J_z)
 =[\langle E_z,D_\tau J_z\rangle]_0^1
 =-\langle\xi,k\rangle_x.$$ For $a\in I(x)$ near $z$, <a href="#lem:hessian-index" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:hessian-index">4</a> can be tested on $E_a+\lambda J_a$. Minimizing the resulting quadratic polynomial in $\lambda\in\mathbb R$ gives $$\begin{split}
 A_x(a)(\xi,\xi)
 &\leq Q_a(E_a,E_a)
       -\frac{Q_a(E_a,J_a)^2}{P(a)}\\
 &\leq C_0-\frac{c_0}{P(a)},
 \qquad c_0>0.
 \end{split}
 \label{eq:trial-hessian}$$ The last inequality holds in one fixed neighborhood: smoothness bounds $Q_a(E_a,E_a)$ from above and bounds $Q_a(E_a,J_a)^2$ away from zero there.

*Reduction to a one-dimensional kernel.* For each fixed $\xi\perp e$, <a href="#lem:transverse-concavity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:transverse-concavity">6</a> on the contracted segment gives $$A_x(rz)(\xi,\xi)
 \geq \frac{l_1}{l_0+l_1}A_x(rb_0)(\xi,\xi)
     +\frac{l_0}{l_0+l_1}A_x(rb_1)(\xi,\xi).$$ The two endpoint velocities are nonconjugate. Smoothness of the branch Hessians therefore makes the right-hand side bounded from below as $r\uparrow1$. On the other hand, $P(rz)>0$ and $P(rz)\to P(z)=0$. Inequality <a href="#eq:trial-hessian" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:trial-hessian">[eq:trial-hessian]</a> would contradict this lower bound if $\langle\xi,k\rangle_x\ne0$. It follows that every $k\in\ker d_z\exp_x$ is orthogonal to $e^\perp$, and hence belongs to $\mathop{\mathrm{span}}\{e\}$. The kernel is nontrivial, so it is exactly $\mathop{\mathrm{span}}\{e\}$. In particular, $e$ is a kernel vector and $z\perp e$.

<a id="eq:singular-upper"></a>
*A joint tangential and radial estimate.* Apply the trial-field construction with $k=\xi=e$, and retain the notation $P$ for this choice. Since $z+te\in G_x$ for small $t$ of both signs, the smooth function $t\mapsto P(z+te)$ has a local minimum of value zero at $t=0$. Its first derivative vanishes, so, for some $\delta>0$ and $C>0$, $$0\leq P(z+te)\leq Ct^2\qquad(|t|\leq\delta).$$ Shrink $\delta$ and choose $r_0<1$ so that both $z+te$ and $r(z+te)$ lie in the fixed neighborhood used in <a href="#eq:trial-hessian" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:trial-hessian">[eq:trial-hessian]</a> whenever $|t|\leq\delta$ and $r_0<r<1$. Choose a Lipschitz constant $L$ for $P$ on a slightly larger ball and a bound $K$ for $|z+te|_x$ in this range. Since $r(z+te)\in I(x)$, we obtain the simultaneous estimate <a id="eq:quadratic-index"></a>$$\begin{split}
 0<P\bigl(r(z+te)\bigr)
 &\leq P(z+te)+L(1-r)|z+te|_x\\
 &\leq Ct^2+LK(1-r)
 \leq C'\bigl(t^2+1-r\bigr).
 \end{split}
 \label{eq:quadratic-index}$$ Combining this with <a href="#eq:trial-hessian" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:trial-hessian">[eq:trial-hessian]</a>, and adjusting positive constants, gives $$A_x\bigl(r(z+te)\bigr)(e,e)
 \leq C_1-\frac{c_1}{t^2+1-r},\qquad c_1>0,
 \label{eq:singular-upper}$$ for all these $t,r$. In particular, the constants do not depend on the manner in which the two parameters approach $0$ and $1$.

<a id="eq:singular-lower"></a>
*The radial identity gives a contradictory lower bound.* Put $B_x(a)=g_x-A_x(a)$ and, for $0<r<1$, define $$f_r(t)=B_x\bigl(r(z+te)\bigr)(z,z),
 \qquad -l_0\leq t\leq l_1.$$ Because $z\perp e$, <a href="#lem:transverse-concavity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:transverse-concavity">6</a> with test vector $z$ and velocity direction $re$ makes $f_r$ convex. By <a href="#eq:radial-hessian" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:radial-hessian">[eq:radial-hessian]</a>, $f_r(0)=0$. Nonconjugacy at $b_1=z+l_1e$ bounds $f_r(l_1)$ from above uniformly as $r\uparrow1$. The convexity inequality on $[0,l_1]$ therefore yields $$f_r(t)\leq\frac{t}{l_1}f_r(l_1)\leq C_2t
 \qquad(0<t\leq l_1)$$ for $r$ close to $1$, with $C_2\geq0$ independent of $r$. The radial identity also says <a id="eq:radial-null"></a>$$B_x(a)(a,\cdot)=0,
 \qquad
 f_r(t)=t^2B_x\bigl(r(z+te)\bigr)(e,e).
 \label{eq:radial-null}$$ Indeed, for $a=r(z+te)$ and $r>0$, the first identity annihilates $z+te$ in either slot. Inserting $z=(z+te)-te$ in both slots gives the second identity, with no factor of $r$ remaining. As $|e|_x=1$, we conclude that $$A_x\bigl(r(z+te)\bigr)(e,e)\geq1-\frac{C_2}{t}
 \qquad(0<t\leq l_1).
 \label{eq:singular-lower}$$ Choose $r=1-t^2$ for sufficiently small positive $t$. Both <a href="#eq:singular-upper" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:singular-upper">[eq:singular-upper]</a> and <a href="#eq:singular-lower" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:singular-lower">[eq:singular-lower]</a> apply and would imply $$1-\frac{C_2}{t}\leq C_1-\frac{c_1}{2t^2}.$$ Multiplying by $t^2$ and letting $t\downarrow0$ is impossible, since $c_1>0$. This contradiction proves the proposition. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-geo-first-cut"></a>
<div id="geo:first-cut" class="corollary">

**Corollary 8** (Nonconjugacy of a minimizing convex hull). *Let $a_1,\ldots,a_m\in T_xM$ be nonconjugate velocities, with $m\geq1$, and suppose $$K=\operatorname{co}\{a_1,\ldots,a_m\}\subset G_x.$$ Then every velocity in $K$ is nonconjugate.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-geo-first-cut"><summary>Proof of stmt-geo-first-cut</summary>

*Proof.* We induct on the number of positive weights in a convex representation. A representation with one positive weight gives one of the prescribed generators. For a representation $$w=\sum_{i=1}^k\theta_i a_i,\qquad
 \theta_i>0,\qquad \sum_{i=1}^k\theta_i=1,\qquad k\geq2,$$ write $$w=(1-\theta_k)w'+\theta_k a_k,\qquad
 w'=\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\frac{\theta_i}{1-\theta_k}a_i.$$ The vector $w'$ is nonconjugate by induction, and $a_k$ is nonconjugate by hypothesis. Their entire segment lies in $K\subset G_x$, so <a href="#prop:segment-nonconjugacy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:segment-nonconjugacy">7</a> makes $w$ nonconjugate as well. Discarding zero weights gives the assertion for every point of $K$. ◻

</details>

In the continuation argument, membership of the entire hull in $G_x$ will follow by taking limits from smaller scales. The corollary then removes conjugacy without assuming convexity of the full minimizing domain.

<a id="sec:geometry"></a>
### Global supports and intermediate transport

We prove the global supporting property by continuing the ordinary subgradient graph from short times to every time below one. The nonconjugacy result of <a href="#sec:conjugacy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:conjugacy">3</a> handles a possible conjugate first cut. A local injectivity argument and covering theory then exclude a second minimizing geodesic at that scale.

We retain the branch Hessian $A_x(w)$ and minimizing domain $G_x$ from <a href="#sec:preliminaries" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:preliminaries">2</a>, and identify tangent vectors and covectors by the metric except when explicitly using coordinate covectors. Put $D=\operatorname{diam}M$ and $$G_x=\{p\in T_xM:d(x,\exp_xp)=|p|\}=\overline{I(x)},
 \qquad c_t=\frac ct,
 \qquad Q_tf(z)=\min_x\{f(x)+c_t(x,z)\}.$$ The total set of minimizing vectors $\{(x,p):p\in G_x\}$ is compact. The total open injectivity domain is open, and it contains a fixed neighborhood of the zero section. We use the usual cut-point alternative: a minimizing vector on its boundary either has a conjugate endpoint or has a second minimizing geodesic to the same endpoint.

A *potential* means a function represented by a continuous datum $v$ as $$u(x)=\max_y\{-c(x,y)-v(y)\}.$$ In <a href="#geo:prelim" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geo:prelim">9</a>, the active logs may be defined using any such representing datum; duality of that datum is not required. When a dual pair is needed, we may replace it by $v(y)=\max_x\{-c(x,y)-u(x)\}$; then $(u,v)$ is a dual pair and $u(x)+c(x,y)+v(y)\geq0$. Additive constants play no role below.

A finite maximum $u(x)=\max_i\{h_i-c(x,y_i)\}$ is included in this definition after dual completion. Indeed, if $v(y)=\max_x\{-c(x,y)-u(x)\}$, then $v(y_i)\leq-h_i$ and $\max_y\{-c(x,y)-v(y)\}\leq u(x)$. An index active at $x$ gives the opposite inequality. This identifies the functions, without asserting that dual completion leaves the set of active targets unchanged.

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
#### Supports, divided action, and subgradients

For a semiconvex function, $\partial u(x)$ denotes its ordinary subdifferential, with vectors identified by the metric. Equivalently, $p\in\partial u(x)$ if, in normal coordinates at $x$, $$u(\exp_xh)\geq u(x)+\langle p,h\rangle+o(|h|).$$ For a fixed uniform semiconvexity constant, the remainder in this inequality can instead be bounded below by a negative quadratic term.

<a id="stmt-geo-prelim"></a>
<div id="geo:prelim" class="lemma">

<a id="geo:active-hull"></a>
**Lemma 9** (Uniform supports and active logs). *There is a geometric constant $C_g$ such that $c(\cdot,y)$ and $c(x,\cdot)$ are uniformly semiconcave, with constant $C_g$, including at cut points. Every potential is $D$-Lipschitz and uniformly semiconvex. If $$\mathcal V_u(x)=\{p\in G_x:
                 u(x)+c(x,\exp_xp)+v(\exp_xp)=0\},$$ then $$\label{geo:active-hull}
 \partial u(x)=\operatorname{conv}\mathcal V_u(x).$$ The total active-log graph $\{(x,p):p\in\mathcal V_u(x)\}$ is compact. Every member of the convex hull in <a href="#geo:active-hull" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="geo:active-hull">[geo:active-hull]</a> has a representation with positive weights using at most $n+1$ affinely independent members of $\mathcal V_u(x)$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-geo-prelim"><summary>Proof of stmt-geo-prelim</summary>

<a id="geo:divided-triangle"></a>
*Proof.* The divided triangle inequality is $$\label{geo:divided-triangle}
 c_{a+b}(z,y)\leq c_a(z,w)+c_b(w,y),\qquad a,b>0.$$ It follows by concatenating constant-speed minimizing curves and using the energy characterization of squared distance. This shortening inequality is also used in (Cordero-Erausquin, McCann, and Schmuckenschläger 2001, Claim 2.4) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[CMS2001](#ref-CMS2001)]. Equality holds when $w$ divides a minimizing geodesic in the time ratio $a:b$.

<a id="geo:split-support"></a>
In particular, if $p\in G_x$, $y=\exp_xp$, and $y_s=\exp_x(sp)$, $0<s<1$, then $$\label{geo:split-support}
 c(z,y)\leq c_s(z,y_s)+\frac{1-s}{2}|p|^2,
 \qquad c_s(x,y_s)=\frac{s}{2}|p|^2.$$ The right side is a smooth upper support near $x$, because $sp\in I(x)$. Taking $s=1/2$, the relevant half-vectors form a compact subset of the open injectivity domain. The supports therefore have a common neighborhood size and a common upper Hessian bound. This proves uniform semiconcavity of the cost; symmetry handles its other variable. Also $|c(x,y)-c(x',y)|\leq Dd(x,x')$. Suprema of the corresponding negative costs consequently give the asserted Lipschitz and semiconvexity bounds for $u$.

<a id="geo:directional-support"></a>
For completeness, the directional derivative at $x$ is $$\label{geo:directional-support}
 u'(x;h)=\max_{p\in\mathcal V_u(x)}\langle p,h\rangle.$$ The lower inequality follows from <a href="#geo:split-support" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="geo:split-support">[geo:split-support]</a> for every active log. To obtain the upper inequality, choose an active endpoint at $x_r=\exp_x(rh)$ and a minimizing log there. Compactness gives, along any convergent subsequence, an active endpoint and a minimizing log at $x$. Applying the uniform upper cost support at $x_r$ to the point $x$ bounds $[u(x_r)-u(x)]/r$ above by the corresponding log paired with $h$, up to $O(r)$. This proves <a href="#geo:directional-support" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="geo:directional-support">[geo:directional-support]</a>. For a semiconvex function, the ordinary subdifferential is the set of linear forms bounded above by every directional derivative: this follows by subtracting the fixed negative quadratic term and applying the supporting-hyperplane characterization of a convex function. Thus <a href="#geo:directional-support" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="geo:directional-support">[geo:directional-support]</a> gives <a href="#geo:active-hull" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="geo:active-hull">[geo:active-hull]</a>. The contact equality defines a closed subset of the compact total minimizing-vector set. Thus the total active-log graph is compact, and so is each of its fibers. Carathéodory’s Theorem gives the finite representation. Choose one with the fewest positive weights: an affine dependence would allow the weights to be varied, preserving their sum and barycenter, until one vanished. Thus its active vertices are affinely independent. ◻

</details>

For a fixed base point $x$, write $$H_s(p)=\frac1s A_x(sp).$$ This is the source Hessian of the divided cost $c_s$ on the branch specified by $sp$. We use it only when $sp$ is nonconjugate; at a cut point the branch is the one defined in <a href="#eq:branch-hessian" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:branch-hessian">[eq:branch-hessian]</a>. The base point $x$ will be clear from the velocity $p$.

<a id="stmt-geo-radial-index"></a>
<div id="geo:radial-index" class="lemma">

<a id="geo:radial-derivative"></a>
<a id="geo:radial-identity"></a>
**Lemma 10** (Radial divided-action identity). *Let $p\in G_x$, and let $0<t<s<1$. Along $\gamma(r)=\exp_x(rp)$, let $S_p(r)$ be the Jacobi tensor with $S_p(0)=0$ and $D_rS_p(0)=\mathrm{Id}$, using parallel orthonormal frames. Then $$\label{geo:radial-derivative}
 \frac{d}{dr}H_r(p)=-S_p(r)^{-1}S_p(r)^{-T},
 \qquad
 H_t(p)-H_s(p)\geq\kappa_g(s-t)\mathrm{Id}$$ for a constant $\kappa_g>0$ depending only on the manifold. Furthermore, $$\label{geo:radial-identity}
 A_x(w)w=w$$ on every smooth minimizing branch.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-geo-radial-index"><summary>Proof of stmt-geo-radial-index</summary>

*Proof.* The Hessian $H_r(p)$ is the index form of the Jacobi field having initial value $\xi$ and final value zero on the time interval $[0,r]$. Let $C_p$ be the other fundamental Jacobi tensor, $C_p(0)=\mathrm{Id}$, $D_rC_p(0)=0$. The field in question is $$J(a)=C_p(a)\xi-S_p(a)S_p(r)^{-1}C_p(r)\xi.$$ Integration by parts identifies its index with $\langle S_p(r)^{-1}C_p(r)\xi,\xi\rangle$.
